## Measured facts
- **The scorer and the verifier now read a page's text from the same source (step 19, 2026-11-26, PROPOSED D-098).** `eval/score.py` verifies a quotation through `corpus.page_text` (PDF text; indexed OCR text when the page has under 40 non-space characters) instead of `pymupdf` directly, with the cited path's basename and the existence check kept. Measured before the change: **1 of 59** citations in the step-17 run differed between the two readers - **q003**, p. 15 of `1975_Cost_cutting_caste_and_community_…pdf`, whose raw text is **0 characters** and whose OCR text holds the quote. After re-scoring every restatable run and diffing per question against the step-18 files: **only q003 moves**, in the **eight runs** that carry that document - **step 17 42 -> 43/55 (dev 36/45, held-out 7/10), citation 38/48, numeric 38/43, MRR 0.839, recall@5 45/48, nf(a) 5/6, nf(b) 46/48**, `repro-a` 39 -> 40, step 16 42 -> 43, step 15 41 -> 42, corrected-gold 39 -> 40, english-only 37 -> 38, wave-01 37 -> 38 - and the other 15 runs are identical. **D-086 met** (q040 + q003 = 2 of 3); **wave-01 cost 1 question** (pre-wave 41 vs `repro-a` 40). Gates exit 0 (dev vs predecessor and `repro-a`, and `--milestone`); `eval/test_scorer.py` 36/36. **Not measured:** how many OCR pages a real user's citations would touch (222 OCR pages exist; the canary touches one), and whether an OCR error could certify a quote the PDF image does not show - it can, and it already could before this change.
- **The citation contract accepts a verified extra page, capped at two, and the whole history is restated on it (step 18, 2026-11-26).** `eval/score.py::MAX_EXTRA_CITATIONS = 2`; `citation_ok` is `bool(citations) and gold_cited and quotes_ok and extra <= 2`, where `extra` counts citation entries whose `(file, page)` is not in `gold_sources` and `quotes_ok` checks **every** citation, extras included, against the page's text after whitespace normalisation. `extra_citations` is stored per question (`None` when the question is unanswerable) and per set as `{total, max, answers}`. Measured by re-scoring 23 stored runs with the shipped scorer: **step 17 38 -> 42/55** (citation 33/48 -> 37/48), **`2026-11-24-repro-a` 35 -> 39** (dev 28/45 -> 32/45), step 15 37 -> 41, step 16 38 -> 42, `corrected-gold` 35 -> 39, pre-wave 38 -> 41, wave-01 34 -> 37, `s6-4` 35 -> 42, pre-step-15 runs +4/+5; recall@5, MRR, numeric and not-found identical in every run. The flips since step 15 are always q017, q039, q040, q049; the cap removes **q049** from the six runs where its answer cited three extras and **q026/q044** from `s6-4`, and nothing since step 15. **Wave-01 cost 3 -> 2.** `eval/test_scorer.py` 33/33 (six new cases). Gates exit 0 on dev and `--milestone`. **Not measured:** any effect on a corpus other than this one, and whether a future answer would stay under the cap. **D-086: q040 only.**
- **The exact refusal line is counted when the answer cites nothing (Step 15, 2026-11-25, PROPOSED D-090).** `generation/verify.py::refused_with_line()` accepts two forms: the line as the whole answer (`line_only`, the old contract) and the line as a whole line with **no `[n]` marker anywhere** in the answer (`exact_line_with_prose`). `said_not_found()` is unchanged, so no paraphrase can become a refusal; `gate.resolve` only adds a reason naming the form. Prompt `s6-3` byte-unchanged, so no cache key moves, and that is safe because an accepted refusal was `unverified` before and unverified answers are never cached. Measured on the accepted set (`eval/runs/2026-11-25-step15-refusal-line/`): **37/55 (dev 30/45, held-out 7/10)** vs **35/55**, **not-found (a) 3/6 -> 5/6**, not-found (b) 46/48 -> 45/48, citation 32/48, numeric 37/43, recall@5 45/48, MRR 0.839, p50 12.2 s / p95 46.7 s. q021 and q055 pass; **q026 and q028 are unchanged** (they cite a page while disclaiming one); **q027 is misfiled** as a refusal because it never cites anything - its answer text is byte-identical in both runs and it failed in both, so the cost is a label, and that is what the written q027 explanation says. Dev gate: **fails rule 1 unexplained, passes with the explanation**; held-out milestone gate passes clean. **Not measured:** whether the marker guard generalises beyond this 55-question set, and any wave-level effect. **D-086: q055 only.**
- **The parse watchdog now scales with `--jobs`, and `--jobs 1` is bit-for-bit unchanged (2026-11-23, PROPOSED D-084).** `ingest/pipeline.py::parse_timeout_for(jobs, timeout)` returns an explicit `timeout` when one is given and otherwise `900 x max(1, jobs)`; `parse_stage` resolves it after clamping `jobs`, so a wave gets **7,200 s per child at 8** instead of 900 s, while `--jobs 1` still gets exactly **900 s**. This is the fix for the measured loss in the wave bullet below - three OCR-heavy reports needed **953 / 1,255 / 1,068 s** at 8-way, and the slowest 8-way document so far (1,749.6 s) sits at 24% of the new cap. `--parse-timeout` keeps its meaning and its number wins; the CLI default is now `None` ("scale with jobs") rather than the literal 900, so `build --help` says so. Verified: `parse_timeout_for(1) == 900.0`, `parse_timeout_for(8) == 7200.0`, `parse_timeout_for(8, 45) == 45.0`, both sub-parsers report `parse_timeout is None` unless passed; `ingest/test_pipeline.py` **28/28** with the new case `the parse watchdog scales with jobs`, `ui/test_pipeline.py` **7/7**. The hung-child test's own cap went **2 s -> 10 s** because a healthy child spends **1.28 s** importing the pipeline before OCR starts, so the 2 s cap killed all three documents on this machine before the change and passes now; the behaviour under test (a hung child is killed, recorded `timeout: no result after N s; killed it`, and not retried) is unchanged. Throwaway `data/tmp-*` corpora/indexes and the failed-run logs (`logs/par-wave2-j8.*`, `logs/par-wave3-j8.*`, `logs/par-wave-j8.*`, `logs/waves-par-wave-j8.jsonl`) were deleted on the owner's instruction - all gitignored, ~450 MB; **`logs/par-wave4-j8.{out,err,done}` is kept** as the evidence for the wave measurement. Extraction, prompts and the index are untouched, so no eval applies and none was run.
- **A finished 119-document wave at `--jobs 8` takes 48 m 04 s and gains 1.22x, not 8x, because contention makes each document ~6x slower (2026-11-23).** The run the owner asked for, uninterrupted on throwaway paths: `add-batch --from data/raw/incoming-wave-pilot --wave pilot-j8c --corpus data/tmp-wave4-corpus --index data/tmp-wave4-index --stages scan,parse,assemble,chunk,embed --jobs 8 --parse-timeout 3600` -> **added 119, hardlink 119, 209,141,991 bytes**, build **2,881.3 s**, wall **2,884 s (48 m 04 s)**, exit 1 for the one known native crash (`crash: exit code 3221225477`, the 2023 Small Area Estimation report). Stages: parse 118 parsed / 1 failed in **2,248.3 s**; assemble **118 documents / 2,275 pages / 145 OCR pages**; chunk **8,910 chunks**; embed 118 documents / 8,910 chunks in **601.4 s**. Pages and chunks are identical to Step 11's serial build, so the 8-way parse changes no output. The children really do overlap - the **sum of the 118 per-document parse seconds is 17,280.9 s against 2,248.3 s wall = 7.69x** - but every document is slower under load (Step 12's serial sum for the same documents was 2,871.6 s, so a document costs ~6x more wall time): the 198-page annual report took **1,749.6 s at 8-way vs 310.4 s serial**, the 120-page wetland proceedings 1,742.5 s, and three 20-page OCR-heavy 1975 reports 953-1,255 s. Wall parse throughput is **60.7 pages/min** against 122.2 pages/min on the 20-document sample, so the **wave-level five-stage build is 3,528 s -> 2,884 s = 1.22x** (Step 11's serial five-stage build of the same 118 documents is the only serial baseline and it included a crash restart). **`--parse-timeout 900` is too tight for `--jobs 8`:** an earlier run of this wave lost three documents to `timeout: no result after 900 s` at real times of **953.2 / 1,255.3 / 1,068.2 s**; at **3,600 s** all 119 finished. Live index untouched (still **187 documents, 186 embedded, 1 failed**), `git status` clean; `logs/par-wave4-j8.{out,err,done}` hold the run. **Not measured:** a 2,000-document wave (this is 119 files, and the heavy tail is a large share of it), and any eval - retrieval, prompts and the index are unchanged, so the `EVAL.md` gate does not apply.
- **A wave no longer pays for the bibliography: the default stage list is five stages (2026-11-23, Step 14, PROPOSED D-083).** `build` and `add-batch` default to **`scan,parse,assemble,chunk,embed`**, by function default and by parsed command line, so `--jobs 8` waves no longer wait on metadata. The removed cost is measured, not estimated: **8.2 s/document** (866.3 s for 106 documents with Ollama up, Step 11), which is **45.1 h** for the 19,813 documents still to come - more than the 8-way parse (~35 h). The stage is unchanged and still runs when named (`--stages ...,bibliography,...`) or alone (`python -m ingest.bibliography build`, which re-ran on the live index in **0.0 s** and left **214 records / 177 reference lines / 2 errors** as they were). A document with no record gives `reference: null` in `ui/pipeline.py::sources()` and the client's existing `source.reference || source.label || source.doc` (`ui/static/app.js:387`) shows the file-name label, so the rail loses no source. `ingest/test_pipeline.py` **27/27** (new case `the bibliography is on demand by default` reads the real defaults and command lines, and the UI suite's existing case already asserts `reference is None` for a document with no record), `ui/test_pipeline.py` **7/7**. Extraction modules untouched, live index unchanged (**187 documents, 186 embedded, 1 failed**), so no eval applied. Also fixed: the parse ETA divided by `jobs` even though `position` already counts completed documents, so an 8-wide run printed an ETA ~8x too low. **Not measured at the time of this entry:** a *finished* wave at `--jobs 8` (measured 2026-11-23 - see the first bullet). The run started 2026-11-22 22:00 (`add-batch --wave pilot-j8 --stages scan,parse,assemble,chunk,embed --jobs 8`, throwaway corpus/index) reached **89 of 119** documents of the parse stage by 22:26 and stopped when that session ended - the background job did not survive it (no `.done` marker, log last written 22:26:16, no worker processes left), so there is **no wall-clock total** and it is not a wave-level figure. Its per-document times (52.9-67.5 s each at 8-wide) are consistent with Step 13's 1.41x; the 200 MB throwaway corpus and 11 MB index were deleted.
- **A wave now parses 8 documents at once, and the shipped `build` measures **1.41x** on real files (2026-11-22, Step 13, PROPOSED D-082).** `parse_stage(..., jobs=N)` submits each document's `parse-one` child to a thread pool and **consumes the futures in plan order**, so the manifest rows, near-duplicate decisions, counters and assembled `pages.json` are identical to a serial run. `build --jobs` and `add-batch --jobs`, default **1** (today's output unchanged). Measured through the real `build` on 20 corpus documents / 296 pages (stages `scan,parse`, throwaway hardlink corpora under `data/tmp-par-jobs`, deleted afterwards): **`--jobs 1` 205.6 s = 86.4 pages/min, `--jobs 8` 145.3 s = 122.2 pages/min = 1.41x**, page totals **296 = 296**; a direct-child sample of the same 20 documents measured **202.2 s -> 132.2 s = 1.53x**. Past 8 there is no gain (4x2 / 8x1 / 8x2 / 16x1 = 41.1 / 43.7 / 43.2 / 43.9 s vs 72.0 s serial), so 8 is the knee and this is not an 8x speedup. `ingest/test_pipeline.py` **26/26** (3 new cases: `jobs=8` matches `jobs=1` row for row, a crash stays isolated under `jobs=3`, `jobs=0` is refused). Extraction untouched (`git diff` empty on `ingest/corpus.py`, `ingest/structure.py`, `ingest/tables.py`), live index untouched (**187 documents, 186 embedded, 1 failed**). **Not measured:** a 2,000-document wave at `--jobs 8` (the two samples are 20 documents), and any eval, because retrieval, prompts and the index are unchanged.
- **Parsing in parallel buys 1.53x, not 8x, and dropping bibliography turns the 20k build from ~200 h into ~41 h (2026-11-22, remembered in `reports/20k-download-and-rag-guide.md` §6.1).** Measured with the shipped `parse-one` child on 20 real corpus documents (296 pages, every 9th file): one at a time **202.2 s = 87.9 pages/min**, 8 at once **132.2 s = 134.3 pages/min = 1.53x**, identical page counts. More workers do not help: on an 8-document sample 4x2 / 8x1 / 8x2 / 16x1 threads gave **41.1 / 43.7 / 43.2 / 43.9 s** against 72.0 s serial, so contention saturates near 1.7x and PyMuPDF's ONNX table detector ignores `OMP_NUM_THREADS`. Embedding is already at its knee: 600 real chunks took **61.7 ms/chunk at 1 torch thread, 18.8 ms at 8, 17.7 ms at 16** (the default 8), which is why the pilot's 65.6 ms/chunk is mostly LanceDB writes and per-document overhead. Table detection is **22%** of a parse (7.3 s of 32.6 s over 41 pages). Projected at these rates for 285,455 pages (14.27 pages/doc): parse **35.4 h**, embed **5.8 h**, bibliography **45.1 h** - so `--stages scan,parse,assemble,chunk,embed` plus an 8-way parse is **~41 h (1.7 days)** against the guide's original **~200 h (8.3 days)**. **Not measured:** 8-way parsing does not exist yet (`parse_stage` is a serial loop, `ingest/pipeline.py:368`), the 20k corpus itself, and wave-level speedup where the tail matters less; the 1.53x is one 20-document sample on this machine, not a 2,000-document run.
- **The 20k source tree is 22,710 PDFs in 31 leaf folders, and ten cursor waves reach 20,000 without re-downloading anything (2026-11-22).** `ingest.drive plan --folder-id 1ew-Pj4ry2vIzYpFMA3h3LiGZT-87YvnP --per-category 999999 --dest data/raw/__count-only` printed **`22710 PDFs to fetch`**; `plan` only lists, so nothing was downloaded and no download approval was consumed. Per folder: min 3, median 195, max 5,500 (`reports/CGIAR`; then `papers/13_Agronomy_Crop_Science` 2,694, `papers/15_Health_Food_Microbiology` 1,869, `papers/12_Policy_Governance` 1,749, `reports/ICIMOD` 1,626, `papers/16_Other` 1,604). The target is 20,000 - 187 = **19,813 new**; `--per-category 2649` plans 19,814. Because `plan()` counts the PDFs already in the destination leaf folder, one `--dest data/raw/bulk` tree plus a rising cursor is a wave with no repeat download: cursors 85/197/348/502/680/880/1087/1394/1795/2822 plan 2,012/4,016/6,020/8,022/10,025/12,025/14,029/16,032/18,032/20,032 cumulatively. **Not measured:** any fetch, so the guide's ~11 h and ~24 GB are the pilot's 29.6 files/min and 1.22 MB/file applied arithmetically, not a run.
- **A cross-encoder rerank of the top 10 restores the wave-01 canary total but fails the gate, so it ships off (2026-11-22, D-081).** `retrieval/rerank.py` (`CHAT_RERANK`, `CHAT_RERANK_DEPTH` 10, `CHAT_RERANK_ANCHOR` to keep BM25's first pages) re-scores 10 pages with `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` and keeps the route's `k`; `generation/answer.py` retrieves `max(k, depth)` pages when it is on, and the cache key carries `+rerank10` (or `+rerank10a1`) so a reranked answer is never replayed with the flag off. Two full runs on the English-only corpus: **unanchored (`eval/runs/2026-11-22-rerank-top10/`) 37/55 (dev 30/45, held-out 7/10), recall@5 44/48, MRR 0.769, citation 35/48, numeric 36/43, not-found (a) 3/6, (b) 43/48, p50 11.7 s / p95 23.7 s; anchored at 1 (`eval/runs/2026-11-22-rerank-anchor1/`) 37/55, recall@5 44/48, MRR 0.836, citation 34/48, numeric 36/43, not-found (a) 4/6, (b) 44/48, p50 12.3 s / p95 38.5 s.** Both **fail `eval/gate.py`** (16 and 13 problems against both the accepted 37/55 run and the English-only run): 5-6 dev questions pass -> fail with no explanation (anchored: q002, q025, q027, q046, q047), not-found (b) -2, unanchored MRR -0.034. On the 8 questions wave 01 broke it is clearly better than the corpus as it now stands - **5 of 8 pass (4 verified + q019's correct refusal) versus 2/8** - because the tail really is where the distractors sit (q002's `1970_Adhikari_Tehri_Dam…` is gone from its list). The loss is on the table route: q025-q027 and q047 keep the right document but the reranker replaces BM25's own top page with a worse one. Retrieval-only screen (`scripts/measure-rerank.py`, no model calls): bm25 **45/48, MRR 0.839**; rerank10 44/48, 0.769; anchor1 44/48, 0.836; anchor2 44/48, 0.834; anchor3 44/48, 0.832. Cost: **9.9 s** to load the model once, **409 ms p50 / 475 ms max** for 10 passages on CPU. `retrieval/test_rerank.py` **10/10**, `generation/test_answer.py` 5/5. Flag off by default, so the shipped pipeline is unchanged.
- **The corpus is now English-only: 29 mostly non-Latin documents quarantined, and the canary did not move (2026-11-22, D-080).** On the owner's instruction, every document under **50% Latin letters** (counted over the page text in `index/pages.json`) left `data/corpus` for `data/quarantine/non-english/<same relative path>` - **29 documents, 26 of them under 30%**, 14 from the original 97 and 15 from wave 01 - recorded one JSON line per file (name, both paths, SHA-256, bytes) in `logs/english-only-removed.jsonl`. Nothing was deleted: corpus entries are hardlinks into `data/raw`, which is untouched, so moving them back restores the old corpus. The manifest and LanceDB were pruned to match (**no vector rows left for a quarantined file**; vectors 11,334 = chunks 11,334). Corpus **216 -> 187 PDFs**, index **215 documents / 3,126 pages / 12,136 chunks / 130 MB -> 186 / 2,669 / 11,334 / 120 MB** (assemble + chunk only, 1.6 s; no re-parse). Full run (`eval/runs/2026-11-22-english-only/`): **33/55 passed (dev 26/45, held-out 7/10)**, recall@5 45/48, recall@10 46/48, MRR **0.839**, citation 33/48, numeric 35/43, not-found (a) 2/6, (b) 46/48, p50 12.0 s / p95 28.1 s / max 46.9 s. Against the 216-document run exactly three statuses moved: **q002 unverified -> verified, q015 unverified -> not_found, q040 verified -> unverified** - net zero, and q040's loss is what the D-079 filter experiment predicted to the question. `eval/gate.py` still fails (11 problems vs the accepted 37/55): 7 dev pass-to-fail, citation -3, numeric -2, not-found (a) -1. Rollback: `backups/2026-11-22-post-wave01` restores the index if a restore is needed, and the quarantine log names every file.
- **Filtering wave 01 by language does not recover the canary, and the cause is not the Nepali documents (2026-11-22).** The 8 dev pass-to-fail questions were run through the real `generation.answer` against four index variants in one process (no rebuild; `answer(index=...)` takes the page index directly). Statuses per variant, q002/q003/q019/q028/q039/q040/q046/q052: **A pre-wave 97 pages** = `verified, verified, not_found, unverified, unverified, verified, unverified, verified` (exactly the accepted baseline, so the harness is faithful); **B current 216** = `unverified, verified, unverified, unverified, unverified, verified, unverified, unverified` (exactly the wave-01 run); **C current minus every document under 50% Latin (185 docs / 2,669 pages)** = `verified, verified, unverified, unverified, unverified, unverified, unverified, unverified` — it returns **q002 only** and **loses q040**, which passes today; **D pre-wave plus English wave documents (199 docs)** behaves like C. So the drop is **not** caused by Devanagari content: every wave document that takes a passage slot in a failing question is English — `1970_Adhikari_Tehri_Dam…` (q002), `1975_Cost_cutting_caste…` (q003), `1970_Bajracharya_Changing_Climatic_Parameters…` (q019, two pages), `2015_Assessment_of_existing_and_potential_landslides…` (q040), `1970_Giri_English_Language_Teachers…` (q052). The gold page is **still rank 1** in the 216-document index for 7 of the 8 (q052 moves 2 -> 1), so this is not a retrieval failure: q002 keeps its same four gold pages at ranks 1-4 and fails because the **5th slot** changed; **q028, q039 and q046 have no wave document in their top-6 at all** and moved only because BM25's corpus statistics (IDF and length normalisation) shift when 2,275 pages are added. Evidence: `artifacts/wave01/wave01-filter-test.log` and `.json` (gitignored; the numbers are also here).
- **Wave 01 added 119 pilot PDFs to the live corpus and the canary dropped 4 questions (2026-11-22).** `add-batch --wave 01 --no-build` reported **added 119, unchanged 0, skipped [], refused []**, 209,141,991 bytes, 119 hardlinks; `build` then took **4,200 s** for 118 documents (parse 118 / chunk 118 / embed 118, bibliography 118), leaving **1 failed** on the known `crash: exit code 3221225477`, **0 pending**, **0 duplicates**. Index: **215 documents / 3,126 pages / 12,136 chunks / 130 MB**, 231 OCR pages, 26 documents carrying unreadable pages. Corpus: **216 PDFs** in `data/corpus` (97 + 119). Evaluation (`eval/runs/2026-11-22-wave01-full/`): **33/55 passed (dev 26/45, held-out 7/10)**, recall@5 45/48, recall@10/30 46/48, MRR **0.837**, citation 33/48, numeric 35/43, not-found (a) 2/6, (b) 47/48, p50 12.4 s / p95 32.9 s. Against `eval/runs/2026-11-20-step9-retry-guard`: passed -4, citation -3, numeric -2, not-found (a) -1, recall unchanged, MRR +0.011; seven statuses changed, all to `unverified`. `eval/gate.py` fails rules 1-4. **Not measured:** any wave-specific gold questions (owner-only eval set) and any per-wave ingestion timing beyond this one run.
- **15 of the 118 wave-01 documents are mostly non-Latin, and the pipeline is English-only (2026-11-22).** Measured from `index/pages.json` by counting ASCII letters per document: of the **118** added documents, **15** are under 50% Latin and **12** under 30% (Nepali/Devanagari: the 2025 National Tiger Survey release, the national parks act, MoFE and NPC policy papers, DHM releases). The pre-existing 97 included 14 such documents. Retrieval is English BM25 plus `bge-small-en-v1.5`, so an English question cannot match them: `"What did Nepal's 2025 National Tiger Survey find?"` refuses although that file is indexed with 2 readable pages of Devanagari text (2059 and 1851 characters). This is a corpus-content limit, not a bug in the wave.
- **The parse child is bounded by a wall-clock watchdog (2026-11-21).** Default `PARSE_TIMEOUT` is **900 s**, ~3x the slowest measured document (310.4 s, the 198-page annual report); `build` and `add-batch` take `--parse-timeout` to raise it. A killed child leaves the row `failed` with `timeout: no result after N s; killed it`, is not retried unless the file's SHA-256 changes, and its partial shards are never assembled because the row never reaches `parsed`. Measured with a **5 s** cap on two real pilot files: both recorded the timeout and the run completed; with the default cap a 2-page release parsed in **11.9 s, exit 0**. `ingest/test_pipeline.py` **23/23**.
- **Parse isolation on the real pilot (2026-11-21, Step 12).** 119 files, the crashing NSO report still staged, stages `scan,parse,assemble`, throwaway corpus/index: **48 m 03 s** wall, **118 parsed, 1 failed, 0 pending**, and the failed row is the access violation (`crash: exit code 3221225477` = 0xC0000005) rather than a lost batch. The run reached **119/119** after the crash; a second identical `add-batch` parsed **0** documents (crash rows are not retried) and assembled **118 documents / 2,275 pages / 145 OCR pages**, which is exactly the Step 11 totals. Per-document cost of the isolation: **1.04 s** to start the child and import the pipeline, measured on the same three documents at **5.2 / 32.6 / 5.0 s** against **4.4 / 31.2 / 3.4 s** for the in-process path — about 1 s, i.e. ~4% of the 22.5 s/document parse measured in Step 11. Sum of the 118 recorded per-document seconds is **2,871.6 s**; median document **6.5 s**, slowest **310.4 s**. **Not measured:** any eval (retrieval, prompts and the index are untouched, so the `EVAL.md` gate does not apply).
- **The 200-document pilot ran on real Drive data (2026-11-21, Step 11).** Plan with `--per-category 7` selected **212 PDFs in 32 leaf folders**; `fetch` landed **210** in **426 s** (257,056,503 bytes, 29.6 files/min) with 2 same-batch duplicate names skipped, each row carrying a SHA-256 in `logs/drive-pilot.jsonl`. **91** staged files were byte-identical to documents already in `index/manifest.sqlite` and were removed, leaving **119** (209,141,991 bytes). `add-batch --wave pilot --from data/raw/incoming-wave-pilot --corpus data/tmp-pilot-corpus --index data/tmp-pilot-index` mirrored 119, parsed 103 in **2,187 s**, then hit the PyMuPDF crash above (exit 139). After excluding that document, the resumed `build` finished **exit 0 in 1,341 s**: parse 15 docs / 472.7 s, assemble 118 docs / **2,275 pages** / 145 OCR'd pages, chunk **8,910 chunks**, embed 118 docs / 8,910 chunks / 584.6 s, manifest **118 embedded, 0 failed, 0 pending**. Combined **3,528 s = 29.9 s/document**, 1.55 s/page (38.7 pages/min including embed and chunk), parse alone 1.17 s/page (51.4 pages/min), embed 15.2 chunks/s. Chunk ratio **3.92 chunks/page**; pilot documents are larger than the corpus average (**19.3 pages and 1.76 MB** versus 8.77 and 0.505). Bibliography for the 118 ran with Ollama down, so all records carried `ConnectError` and its 265 s is a failed-call timing; the 106 error records were then deleted and re-extracted with Ollama up (`qwen3.5:4b`, `bib-3`, temp 0, seed 0) in **866.3 s = 8.17 s/document**, leaving **1 of 118 in error** (an IFPRI annual report whose JSON was cut off at `num_predict` 220) and **99 records with a reference line** (78 titles, 75 author lists, 89 years, 57 venues, 10 DOIs). The earlier 12-document sample (6.9 s/doc) understated this by ~19%. `ingest.pipeline status` on the live index printed **`"documents": 97`, `"embedded": 97`, `"failed": 0`**. The temp corpus/index are ignored under `data/` and can be deleted at any time.
- **A real test batch went through `add-batch` end to end in 40 s, and the live corpus was not touched (2026-11-21).** Three synthetic 2-page PDFs were staged at `data/raw/incoming-wave-test/` (owner-requested; the folder is still there and is not in the corpus) and the real CLI was run against a throwaway corpus and index so the 97-document index stayed as it was: **added 3, unchanged 0, refused 0, 3 hardlinks, 4,430 bytes**, build **38.6 s** for 3 documents / 6 pages (most of it three bibliography model calls at ~7 s and loading the embedder; 6 chunks embedded), wall **40 s**, all three `embedded` in the temp manifest, three rows in `logs/wave-additions.jsonl`, `meta` key `wave-test` = `{"documents": 3, "bytes": 4430, ...}`. A rerun with `--no-build` reports **added 0, unchanged 3, refused 0**. `ingest.pipeline status` on the live index still reports **97 documents, 97 embedded**, so nothing leaked into the real corpus. **Not measured:** a 200-document pilot batch (the step's last box).
- **Retrieval cost scales linearly with pages, and the first real trigger is around 9,000 documents, not 20,000 (2026-11-21).** `index/pages.json` and the page BM25 were measured on the real 851-page index and on a synthetic 1,702-page version of it (every page duplicated under a `2x/` document name, so token counts per page are unchanged and only the corpus size differs). Separate processes, 55 real eval questions each:

  | | 1x (97 docs / 851 pages) | 2x (194 / 1,702) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `pages.json` bytes | 2.30 MB | 4.65 MB |
  | JSON load | 0.008 s | 0.027 s |
  | BM25 build | 0.13 s | 0.21 s |
  | RSS after BM25 / peak after queries | 73 / 93 MB | 95 / 133 MB |
  | BM25 top-5 latency | p50 2.54 ms, p95 3.18 ms | p50 5.15 ms, p95 7.16 ms |

  Both latency and the BM25 index grow linearly (query latency doubles when pages double), so extrapolating the measured per-page cost puts the plan's **p95 > 300 ms trigger at ~80,000 pages (~9,100 documents)** and the **2 GB RSS trigger at ~81,000 pages (~9,200 documents)** - early in wave 4-5, not near 20k. Extrapolation, not a 20k measurement: the 1x/2x points are measured, the rest is linear arithmetic. This is the strongest argument yet for the FTS5/sharding step in `reports/20k-ingestion-plan.md` section 6, and the plan is updated to say so.
- **`add-batch` brings a staged folder in all-or-nothing, and it is measured on 19 cases (2026-11-21).** `python -m ingest.pipeline add-batch --from data/raw/incoming-wave-NN --wave NN` walks the folder, then refuses the whole batch before touching the corpus when a file is not a PDF (Word files point at the pinned-LibreOffice name), is outside `data/raw` or already in the corpus, repeats a name inside the batch, or duplicates a batch name that the corpus has with different bytes. **A file whose bytes are already indexed, or repeat an earlier file in the batch, is skipped and reported** (`skipped: [{name, reason}]`) instead of failing the batch, per the owner's 2026-11-21 request, so a re-fetched folder that overlaps the corpus is harmless. Accepted files are hardlinked with their folder relative to `data/raw` (copy on `os.link` failure), logged one row per file to `logs/wave-additions.jsonl`, and the wave is recorded in the manifest's existing `meta` table (`wave-NN` -> documents/bytes/time, no schema migration), then `build` runs. `ingest/test_pipeline.py` **19/19** (12 existing + 7 new): links and indexes two nested files, refuses a repeated in-batch name with **nothing placed**, refuses a name the corpus has with different bytes (the corpus file is byte-unchanged), skips the same bytes under another name and still lands the rest of the batch, reports `unchanged` on a rerun, refuses a folder outside `data/raw` and a `.docx` batch, and copies when `os.link` is patched to fail. CLI check: a missing `--from` prints `refused` and exits **1**. **Not measured:** the wall time of a real batch, because no wave folder is staged (the step's last box).
- **The reference line under an answer now opens its cited page (2026-11-21, owner request).** It used to be plain text (the earlier choice to keep `[n]` from repeating as a chip), so the line a reader clicks at the bottom of an answer did nothing. `ui/static/app.js` now turns each line into one button carrying the verifier's own file, page and quote (`verification.citations[n]`, falling back to the retrieved source), and `ui/static/citation-viewer.js` labels its outbound link **Open original PDF** at the page on screen. Verified in Chrome by `scripts/ui-starters-check.mjs`, which now clicks each reference line as well as each chip: **4/4 starter cards PASS**, 1/1 reference per card opened the cited page with the quoted text highlighted, the outbound link ended `#page=<n>`, **0 console errors** (`artifacts/step9/ui-starters-check.json`). No server, pipeline, prompt or index change; commit `1aac4a3`.
- **Every starter card and citation chip was checked in the browser (2026-11-20).** `scripts/ui-starters-check.mjs` drives the installed Chrome over CDP, clicks each of the four empty-state cards in a fresh chat, waits for the answer and then opens every citation chip it produced. The first run found three bad cards: "average bamboo yield per hectare" and "how many Terai households in bamboo taboos" are questions the corpus does not answer, and the Watanabe glacier card answered but settled `unverified`, so its chips opened no highlighted page. All three are replaced with questions from the passing eval set (the 1993 return period, q005/q037; Kerabari's elephant damage, q052) and the check passes: **4/4 cards verified, every chip (6 of 6) opens its page with the quote highlighted, 0 console errors**, twice in a row. The missing favicon that logged a 404 on every run is fixed with an inline icon, and the shared CDP plumbing moved to `scripts/ui-cdp.mjs` so the step-7 check and this one are one copy.
- **A verification retry no longer runs the context dry, and it is the second kept change (2026-11-20).** q047 measured the failure: after its first answer failed the citation check, the retry prompt was **4,063 of 4,096** tokens, so the model's second answer was cut at 33 tokens and the question was lost. `generation/answer.py` now reads the counters Ollama returns for the first call and appends the failed answer only while `prompt_tokens + answer_tokens` stays under 85% of `num_ctx`; above that the answer is dropped and the retry message says why. Full run (`eval/runs/2026-11-20-step9-retry-guard/`): **37/55 passed (dev 30/45, held-out 7/10)**, citation **36/48** (35), numeric **37/43** (36), not-found (b) 46/48, (a) 3/6, recall@5 45/48, MRR 0.826, p50 **11.4 s** / p95 **28.9 s**. Dev gate passes (its only rule-1 entries are the inherited q017/q051 extra-page explanations), held-out milestone gate passes. q047 now answers `69.85%` with a verified row quote. Kept as D-072.
- **Adding table rows to the passage was measured and rejected (2026-11-20).** `retrieval.tables.with_rows()` appends a page's cleaned table rows to its passage body, behind `CHAT_TABLE_CONTENT`. On the 13 table-route questions the built prompt measured **16,012 characters** — over the 4,096-token context — so Ollama truncated it and the model refused: q029 and q030 went `verified -> not_found`, q041/q046/q047 `verified -> unverified`, and the truncated calls reported `prompt_eval_count` 2,050. Rejected as D-073; flag off. The constraint this proves is in `reports/next-experiments.md`: on the table route rows must replace the prose window, not be added to it.
- **The UI evidence rail is a toggle only (owner request, 2026-11-20).** An answer used to open it and clicking anywhere on an assistant message did too, which most readers never need and which fought text selection. `ui/static/app.js` now fills the rail only when it is already open, and the message-click shortcut is gone; the rail starts hidden and the `Evidence` button (or a citation chip) is the way in. `scripts/ui-browser-check.mjs` asserts the rail is still hidden after an answer and then opens it by its own button before it inspects the source cards; the run passes (`artifacts/step7/ui-browser-check.json`).
- **The citation prompt now makes each sentence quote its own evidence, and it is the first measured improvement since Step 6 (2026-11-20).** `prompt.PROMPT_VERSION` is `s6-3`: a quotation supports only the sentence carrying its number, two sentences with different facts get their own numbers and quotations, and a figure in a sentence must appear in that sentence's quotation. Full 55-question run (`eval/runs/2026-11-20-step9-cite-evidence/`, table pass off, cache off): **36/55 passed (dev 29/45, held-out 7/10)**, citation **35/48** (31), numeric **36/43** (34), not-found (b) **46/48** (45), not-found (a) unchanged 3/6, recall@5 45/48 and MRR 0.826 unchanged, p50 **13.4 s** / p95 **27.7 s** (16.5 / 46.4). Dev gate: rules 2-4 pass and **rule 1 passes with three written explanations** - q017 and q051 each cite a real extra page whose quotation verifies (the figures are right; D-041 counts the extra page a miss) and q047's retry prompt reached **4,063 of 4,096** tokens so its second answer was cut at 33 tokens. Held-out milestone gate passes (citation 7/9, passed 7/10, MRR 0.806). Kept as D-071 and recorded as the new baseline. Consequence: every cached answer re-asks once, because the cache key holds the prompt version (D-017).
- **The table pass was built, measured and rejected; the diagnosis is the useful part (2026-11-20).** `retrieval/tables.py` scores the 268 cleaned table chunks with the page BM25 and puts up to 2 of their pages into the `table` route's 6 passages, behind `CHAT_TABLE_RETRIEVAL`. The retrieval-only check (`scripts/measure-table-retrieval.py`, `artifacts/step9/table-retrieval-check.md`) showed the gold page is **already rank 1** for q026-q028/q042, so the pass gained **no** gold page on any of the 13 table questions and **dropped q046's**. The full run with the flag on (`eval/runs/2026-11-20-step9-table-retrieval/`) scored 33/55 (dev 27/45, held-out 6/10), citation 31/48, numeric 35/43, MRR 0.823, and the dev gate **failed rule 1** (q047 PASS -> fail). Rejected as D-070; the flag is off by default, so `merge()` is never called and the shipped retrieval is the accepted one. The measurement also says where the row text is actually missing: a page's passage is its running text, not its table.
- **`eval/test_scorer.py` no longer assumes 30 questions (2026-11-20).** Its 10 stale checks came from hardcoded 30-question counts (30 passed, 20 dev, 26 answerable, 5 dev points, q016-q030 regressing); the fixtures had also never been rebuilt (30 rows against a 55-question set) because `make_fixtures.perfect_row` read `gold_sources[0]` before checking `answerable`, which crashes on the newer not-found questions. Both are fixed: the fixtures rebuild when their row count differs from the set, and the test derives its expectations from the gold data **per question** (a perfect row can only pass if `gold_quote` is on the gold page and every `gold_numbers` value appears in `gold_answer`), so it fails when the scorer and the gold data disagree instead of when the set grows. All checks pass; the three questions the gold data cannot make perfect are named with their reason (q042 and q050 spell their number as a word, q044's gold quote lost a hyphen).
- **D-060 was built, measured and rejected; the shipped verifier is unchanged (2026-11-20).** The approved rule (a first sentence that negates what the passages contain counts as a refusal, about the question asked, normalised to `NOT_FOUND_TEXT`) ran over the full 55-question set in `eval/runs/2026-11-20-step9-d060/`: **36/55 passed (dev 31/45, held-out 5/10)**, not-found (a) **6/6**, citation 31/48, numeric 34/43, recall@5 45/48, MRR 0.826, p50 16.4 s / p95 50.8 s. Exactly five statuses changed, all to `not_found`: **q020, q021 and q055 correctly** (those three were refusing in prose) and **q027 and q028 wrongly** (answerable Das Table 2 questions whose model answer opens "The provided passages do not contain Table 2 ..."), so not-found (b) fell **37/39 -> 35/39** on dev and `eval/gate.py` failed rule 1 (two unexplained pass-to-fail questions) plus rules 2 and 3 (a two-question drop); the held-out milestone gate passed with every metric identical. Per Step 9 task 3 the change was removed rather than re-tuned: `generation/verify.py` accepts only the exact first line again, `generation/test_verify.py` is **26/26** with three cases pinning the measured behaviour, all seven generation suites pass, and replaying the shipped verifier over the 2026-11-19 answers changes **0 of 55** statuses, so the accepted run still describes the shipped pipeline. Recorded as D-068, with the real cause named as table retrieval (D-069).
- **`RUNBOOK.md` exists and was followed from scratch (2026-11-20).** Nine sections: start (model, UI, CLI), stop, add documents (with the D-027 not-found re-check, the pinned LibreOffice conversion and the offline check), rebuild, backup, restore, verify, troubleshooting, known limits. Every command was run in order and the output kept in `artifacts/step9/runbook-test.log`: `status` 97 documents / 97 `embedded` / 0 failed; `build` 6.9 s with nothing changed; backup written and restored into a scratch folder with `pages.json` `395c5a2bd7114890` and `chunks.jsonl` `1208cb59d3896db9` **identical on both sides**; `add-document.ps1` refused a file inside `data/corpus` and a duplicate name with exit 1 and no write; `/api/health` reported 97 documents / 851 pages / 86 OCR pages; one `/api/chat` question came back `status verified` with a `[1]`-cited answer (a cache hit, so the stream carried `start` then `done`, no tokens); the UI bound 127.0.0.1 only and refused connections after it was stopped. Suites re-run from the runbook: `ingest/test_pipeline.py` 12/12, `generation/test_verify.py` 26/26, `generation/test_cache.py` 21/21, `ui/test_pipeline.py` 7/7, `ui/test_server.py` 8/8, and `eval/gate.py` passed on the accepted run.

- **A citation now opens the cited page inside the UI with the quote highlighted (2026-11-19).** `ui/static/citation-viewer.js` is one additive module: a chip or an evidence card opens a modal with the cited PDF page rendered by pdf.js **4.10.38** (vendored, two files + LICENSE, hashes recorded above), at the cited page, with the verified quote marked. The mark is a DOM `Range` built from the page's own text layer, painted as one rectangle per line from `getClientRects()` - nothing is rewritten and no coordinates are guessed. Measured in Chrome: q001's chip opened **page 2 of 5** with **12 highlight rectangles** over "the 24 hour rainfall depth of 540 mm at tistung and 482 mm at ghantemadi were recorded in morning hours of 20 th july"; page 3 opened with **0 highlights** (the quote is not there), back restored them, the evidence card opened the same viewer from the retrieved passage, Escape closed it, **0 console errors**; the not-found path opens nothing and still shows its warning. The matcher requires 75% of the quote's words to align and tolerates pdf.js splitting a token (`20` + `th`), so an OCR'd page whose text differs reports "cited text not found on this page" instead of highlighting the wrong words. Bug found by the check and fixed: the page buttons clamped to page 1 because the total was parsed from the "/ 5" label. No server, pipeline, prompt or index change (`ui/test_pipeline.py` 7/7, `ui/test_server.py` 8/8 unchanged).
- **Two different questions were being served the same answer; both causes are fixed (2026-11-19).** Asking "What was the 24-hour rainfall at Tistung in July 1993?" and then "Bagmati River that had favored severe flooding is only about" returned the Tistung answer twice, the second time marked `cached`. Two defects, both measured with the real pipeline: (a) the answer cache key held only the **condensed** question, so a follow-up the condenser had rewritten into the previous question looked up that question's entry; (b) the condenser really did rewrite it - `condensed True` and `search_question` identical to the previous question - so the pipeline then searched the wrong question and produced the same answer even on a cache miss. Fixes: the key now holds **the question asked and the question searched** (plus index, model and prompt versions), and `generation/condense.py` rejects a rewrite that keeps **none of the question's own content words** (a rewrite must keep at least one word of four or more characters that is not a stopword; "and in 1990?" keeps `1990`). Verified: `generation/test_cache.py` **21/21** (new case replays the exact two questions), `generation/test_condense.py` **11/11** (2 new cases), all seven generation suites pass; through `/api/chat` the second turn now reports `condensed False`, searches "Bagmati River that had favored severe flooding is only about" and answers from the Bagmati page (1845 km²) instead of repeating the Tistung answer. Full 55-question eval re-run with the gate: **recall@5 45/48, MRR 0.826, citation 31/48, numeric 34/43, not-found 3/6 and 45/48, 33/55 passed - 0 status and 0 answer changes, both dev gate rules hold** (`eval/runs/2026-11-19-step8-after-condense-guard/`), because the only two eval follow-ups (q023, q024) keep their own content words and are rewritten as before. Every existing cache entry is keyed differently now, so the first ask of each question re-answers once (a cache, not a corpus change). Still open, and separate: the Tistung answer's 2nd and 3rd sentences are not supported by the quote they cite - that is D-067's measurement, not this bug.
- **D-066 is built: each document gets a validated reference, and the rail shows it (2026-11-19).** `ingest/bibliography.py` reads the header pages (page 1, or the first page with a header block - several ICIMOD reports leave page 1 empty), asks `qwen3.5:4b` for title, authors, year, venue and DOI, and keeps a value only when the page actually prints it; every kept field stores the page text that evidenced it and every dropped field stores why. Result over the whole corpus: **96 of 96 documents in the index** have a record (the 97th corpus file, `2006_Komori_Scars_..._W2328682512.pdf`, has 0 pages and appears in no index artifact) - **title 62, authors 64, year 68, venue 43, DOI 5, a printable reference 78, 1 error**. The error is `2004_national_agricultural_policy_2061_...`: the model answered in legacy-font glyphs and no JSON could be parsed, so that document keeps the DOI/year fallback; the stage is non-fatal by design (`ingest/pipeline.py`, case "the bibliography stage survives a dead model"). Measured: **1,371.5 s** of model calls for the 96 documents (about 14 s each, `logs/bibliography-run.log`), a second pass over nothing in **0.4 s**. In Chrome, q001's rail now reads **"Marahatta & Bhusal (2009). Relating Hydrological Extremes with Area – A Case on Extreme Floods in South Central Nepal"** and **"DHAR (1959). The diurnal variation of rainfall at Barahkshetra and Kathmandu during monsoon months"** instead of two file names, with `p2`/`p4` and the file name below each; 0 console errors. **Known limit, measured:** the check proves the string is on the header page, not that it is the right author - `2001_Li_Water_Induced_Disasters...` yields "Tianchi & Gurung (2001)" because that chapter's header prints those names. Tests: `ingest/test_bibliography.py` 7/7, `ingest/test_pipeline.py` 12/12, `ui/test_pipeline.py` 7/7, `ui/test_server.py` 8/8.
- **Two writes to `index/bibliography.json` at once silently lose records, and are now refused (2026-11-19).** Three `build` processes ran at the same time in this session (each `build` loads the artifact, then rewrites it whole), and the survivor's file was missing 57 records the others had paid for - a later pass had to redo them (**459.5 s**, `logs/bibliography-final.log`). `build()` now takes `index/bibliography.lock` and returns `{skipped: true}` instead of starting a second writer; `--force` takes over a lock left by a killed run. Case: "a second build is refused".
- **The unreadable-pages warning read as one run-on line, and now does not (2026-11-19).** The title was a `<span>` and the counts a `<p>` inside the same box, so the box rendered - and copied - as "Some pages cannot be read8 of 97 documents…". The title is now its own block (`.unreadable-warning__title`, `display: block`, separate margins). The one clause also merged two different page sets - the pages that stayed empty after OCR and the pages OCR did read - so the body now states them as two facts: **"19 of 851 pages, in 8 of 97 documents, still have no readable text after OCR. 86 pages were read by OCR. The answer may simply not be on a readable page."** Measured in Chrome on the not-found question: title "Some pages cannot be read", **title bottom 96 px, body top 98 px** (separate lines), per-document lines "annual 2023 has 3 unreadable pages: 2, 8, 27" and "Li 2001 has 1 unreadable page: 1", 0 console errors. Both numbers re-read from `index/pages.json`: 97 documents, 851 pages, 8 documents / 19 pages unreadable, 86 OCR pages. The browser check now fails unless title and body are on separate lines and the body keeps both counts apart.
- **The citation line under an answer no longer becomes chips, and that is verified in the browser (2026-11-19).** A paragraph that is only `[n] file, p. n: "quote"` is marked `message__ref` and skipped by `linkCitations`, so the reference line stays plain text while the prose keeps its chips. Measured on q001: chips `[1]` (1 chip, brackets intact), `chips in refs 0`, `✓ verified · 1 quote`, 0 console errors, PASS.
- **D-067 prototype: 37 of 92 factual sentences are not fully supported by the quote they cite (2026-11-19, secondary signal, not a gate).** `scripts/measure-entailment.py` re-judged every sentence in the 41 verified answers of `eval/runs/2026-11-19-step8-full/` that carries a `[n]`: 96 sentences, 4 of them cite a number the answer never explains, **92 judged - 55 SUPPORTED, 3 PARTIAL, 34 UNSUPPORTED (40.2% not fully supported)**. The failures are mostly the printed quotation, not the fact: **15 of the 37 state a number the quote lacks but the cited page prints**, **0 state a number that appears neither in the quote nor on the page**, and 22 add no number at all (an inference or a paraphrase beyond the quote). The 4 unexplained markers: q002 [1], q020 [2], q046 [5] and q046 [6]. Judge is `qwen3.5:4b` at temperature 0, seed 0, same model file; every row is in `reports/entailment.jsonl` (sentence, verdict, reason, quote, file, page, numbers outside the quote) and the readable list is `reports/entailment.md`. Re-running it reproduces the same 55/3/34 twice, so the judge is stable on this input; it is still a 4B model, so a verdict is a prompt for a human read, never a pass/fail.
- **The review's "bare 1" complaint was a real UI defect, and it is fixed (2026-11-19).** The answer text carries `[1]`, but `app.js` replaced the marker with a chip whose label was `String(n)`, so on screen a sentence read "… was 540 mm 1." and three sentences citing one page read "1 1 1". The chip now keeps its brackets; the headless browser records the chip text and the check fails unless every chip reads `[n]`. Measured: q001 → chips `[1] [1]`, `✓ verified · 1 quote`, 0 console errors; the not-found question still shows its warning, 0 console errors; `ui/test_server.py` 8/8 and `ui/test_pipeline.py` 6/6 still pass. Artifact: `artifacts/step7/ui-browser-check.json`.
- **The rest of the review does not reproduce on the shipped pipeline, but two of its structural points are real (2026-11-19).** Reproduced q001 and q017 through `generation.answer` with the cache off: every factual sentence carries a bracketed marker, each answer ends with one citation line per number, and each quotation is checked against the cited page (`q001`: "The 24-hour rainfall depth recorded at Tistung on 20 July 1993 was 540 mm [1]."). There were no bare markers and no three-sentence block sharing one unverified citation in either answer. What is real: (a) **21 of 48 answers repeat the same marker in adjacent positions** (one per sentence, which is the prompt's contract, so on screen it looks like "[1] [1] [1]"); (b) **2 of 98 factual sentences carry no marker at all** (q035, q050 - both hedge rather than assert); (c) the verifier checks that quoted text is on the cited page and that stated numbers appear in the cited sentence, **not** that an unquoted sentence is entailed by its citation, so an inference phrased without new numbers passes (q017's third sentence does exactly that); (d) the citation line prints the raw file name, which is a storage key.
- **There is no bibliographic metadata in the index (2026-11-19).** The short label is built from the file name by `corpus.label_for` (`1970_Marahatta_…` → "Marahatta 1970"), which for that document is not its publication year - page 1 reads "44 S. Marahatta & J. K. Bhusal **August 2009**" and the paper is about the July 1993 floods. The index holds no title, authors, journal or DOI, which is why both the answer's citation line and any human-readable reference have to fall back to the file name. Fixing it means extracting metadata once per document at ingestion, not renaming files (D-030 keeps the corpus names).

A second run with nothing changed took **1.6 s** and parsed nothing. Byte identity with the pre-Step-8 artifacts holds for all three: `pages.json` `395c5a2b…`, `parsed/pages.jsonl` `f7e82daf…`, `chunks.jsonl` `1208cb59…`.

Dev gate: every rule holds (recall@5 37/39 both sides, MRR 0.831 both sides). Held-out milestone gate: every rule holds (recall@5 8/9, citation 5/9, not-found (a) 1/1, MRR 0.806 both sides). Latency over the 55 questions: p50 **16.5 s**, p95 **46.4 s**, max 69.8 s; statuses 41 `verified`, 7 `not_found`, 7 `unverified`.

This is also the first clean reproducibility datapoint between two runs: same model files, same prompt, same seeds produced byte-identical answers on every question. Artifacts `eval/runs/2026-11-18-step6-after-ui/`, logs `artifacts/step6/eval-after-ui.log`, `gate-after-ui-dev.log`, `gate-after-ui-heldout.log`.
- **The UI streams the pipeline rather than a copy of it (2026-11-18).** `_ask()` sends the same body, options and seed and only switches `stream` on when a callback is given, so the text is the same text: on q001 the streamed answer was **302 characters in 108 pieces and byte-identical to the non-streamed answer**, both `verified`. Per-stage `timings` on that call: retrieval 6.5 ms, verify 9,516.9 ms (the model wait is now its own `model_wait` field), total 9,523.5 ms. A cached answer reports `cache_lookup` 43.2 ms and total 710 ms with `model_total` still carrying the original generation's Ollama counter.
- **The UI's own checks, measured in the browser (2026-11-18).** Loopback `curl` 200 and **LAN address 192.168.1.67:8000 refused**, so the server is not reachable from another machine. One live question rendered `✓ verified · 1 quote` with a working numeric `[1]` chip and five scored sources (top 52.50, two marked OCR); the Debug tab returned `debug_version` 1 with `route=normal`, `k=5`, `rank`/`rerank_score: null`/`score`, timings, tokens 3199 prompt / 120 answer. A not-found question rendered `∅ not found` with the exact refusal line and the warning **"8 of 97 documents have pages with no text layer (19 of 851 pages, 86 pages read by OCR)"**, matching `index/pages.json`. No console errors on any of it.
- **The prose-number verifier is measured on a real run (2026-11-18).** Full 55-question generation eval through `generation/answer.py` with the shipped verifier: **33/55 passed (dev 28/45, held-out 5/10), citation 31/48, numeric 34/43, not-found (a) 3/6, (b) 45/48**, recall@5 45/48, MRR 0.826 - identical to 2026-11-16 in every metric except numeric, which fell 35/43 to 34/43. One cause, checked by hand: q039 states "P0 is 54 [1]" while the page it cites says 540, and the retry repeated it. Re-checking all 55 saved answers with `verify.prose_number_problems` flags **exactly 1 answer (q039), 0 others**. Both gates pass (dev with that one written explanation, held-out milestone with zero drift). p50 17.1 s / p95 48.2 s, slowest q044 69.0 s. Artifacts `eval/runs/2026-11-18-step6-prose-number-check/`; row in `EVAL.md`.
- **This eval ran from a copy of the question set, not `eval/questions.jsonl`.** The working copy carries 432 appended lines of markdown (hash `30f80ecd…` against the committed `1b540a5d…`), which makes `eval/score.py` and `eval/gate.py` fail with `JSONDecodeError`. The run used `git show HEAD:eval/questions.jsonl` written into the run folder, so the set is byte-identical to the committed one and the protected file was not touched. See Blockers.
- **Prose numbers joined the verifier, measured before it was built (2026-11-17).** Over the 55 saved answers in `eval/runs/2026-11-16-step6-full/`: **114 numbers stated in a sentence that carries a `[n]` marker, of which 1 is missing from every page that sentence cites - 0 false positives.** The one hit is q039's "which is 54 mm" where page 3 of the Marahatta case study says 540 mm; its quotation was genuine, which is why nothing caught it. Re-running the implemented verifier over the same answers gives 42 verified -> 41, 7 not-found, 6 unverified: **1 verified -> unverified (q039), 0 gained, and no change to the recorded score**, because q039 already failed the scorer's citation rule. A live run would spend its one retry on that answer. Digits glued to letters (`P0`, `m3`, file names), `[n]` markers, and sentences with no marker are not numbers for this rule; 8 sentences cite a marker with no citation line and stay with the earlier checks. Evidence: `scripts/measure-prose-numbers.py` (per-number detail, with the first, noisier attempt's causes narrowed) and `scripts/measure-verify-delta.py` (status changes).
- **Step 6 full generation eval measured (2026-11-16): 33/55 passed (dev 28/45, held-out 5/10), citation 31/48, numeric 35/43, not-found (a) 3/6, (b) 45/48.** Every question went through `generation/answer.py` with the cache off (`eval/run_generation.py`, no tuning): `qwen3.5:4b` digest `d8b0f5e9760c`, prompt `s6-2`, temperature 0, seed 0, num_ctx 4096, `think: false`, 97 documents / 851 pages. 42 answers verified, 7 not-found, 6 unverified after the one retry. **Latency p50 16.6 s / p95 58.1 s** (mean 20.4 s excluding the one stalled q038; machine sleep during the run, see Blockers). **Retrieval gate passes:** recall@5 45/48 and MRR 0.826 against the locked 45/48 and 0.829 (one question = 0.021), with the same three misses q015, q045, q046. Failure modes: refusal-format non-compliance (q020, q021, q055), table rows missing from the six passages (q026-q028, q042), and citations that add a non-gold page or paraphrase (17 of 48 answerable). Artifacts `eval/runs/2026-11-16-step6-full/`; rows in `EVAL.md`.
- **One owner for page lookup (2026-11-16):** `corpus.py` now owns page text, page words, the page → file path, the name → corpus-relative path, the corpus signature and the page index. `ui/retrieval.py` was deleted and its four copies of the lookup (verify, ingest verify, chunk, cache) import `corpus` instead. The cache stores one fingerprint per cited page (`text:<sha256>` for text pages, `file:<sha256>` for scans) and `get()` re-checks them, so a removed document, removed page or edited page is a miss. 75 generation cases and 83 chunk cases pass; `eval/run_retrieval.py` after the refactor wrote a retrieval file **byte-identical** to the locked baseline (`FBC3C315…EB94`).
- **Corpus grown from Drive (2026-11-14): 97 PDFs in `data/raw/` and `data/corpus/`, no duplicate basenames.** `ingest/drive.py` walked the shared tree, kept `--per-category 2` PDFs per leaf folder (folders starting with `_` skipped), and fetched **37 new files**; **1 was skipped as a duplicate by content** (a NARAYAN copy already at top level). Hardlinks mirror `data/raw`'s folders into `data/corpus` (D-030). Every download is recorded with its Drive id, byte count, SHA-256 and link mode in `logs/drive-downloads.jsonl`. `data/raw` was not otherwise touched.
- **97-document parse measured (2026-11-14): 851 pages, 242 tables, 86 OCR pages, 20 unreadable pages, 996.1 s (51.3 pages/min; 32.5 h per 100k pages).** Table detection costs 469 ms/page on every text page; text extraction without it runs at 72.1 pages/min; OCR 4.2 s/page. Numeric table tokens: **5,511 of 5,520 match** the page's own words (99.8%). The 9 mismatches are listed in `artifacts/step3/report.md`; four of them are OCR/encoding artefacts (`6j`, `6]lnkmfg]` from a Japanese and a Devanagari scan) and two are the known ice-core cell splits (`.26`, a long zero run). The unreadable share is **2.4% (20 of 851)**, still above the 1-2% rule, even after OCR; 6 of those pages are in the two broken PDFs. Some detected "tables" are false positives on running text (e.g. 2012 non-hasl survey reports 67), which the report counts rather than hides.
- **All five eval source documents are in the 97-document parse** (Marahatta, Ahasan, Das, Watanabe, NARAYAN), so every gold page still resolves.
- **Two corpus PDFs yield no text at all and are reported rather than repaired:** `2005_Choudhary_Trans_boundary_Floods_..._W210886295.pdf` (truncated; pdfplumber raises `Unexpected EOF`, PyMuPDF returns 4 pages with 0 characters) and `2006_Komori_Scars_of_the_Glacial_Lake_Outburst_Flood_..._W2328682512.pdf` (opens with 0 pages). The parser records both and the chunker yields no chunks for them; replacing them means re-downloading, and `data/raw/` is owner-only.
- **Step 4 chunk export on the 97-document parse (2026-11-14): 3,226 chunks from 95 documents (2,958 text + 268 table), 0.5 s.** Metadata: **2,381 chunks carry a year**, **1,633 a publisher** (reports folders only), and **946 carry a real section path**; the rest say `(no section headings)`. **Document contexts are now measured (2026-11-15): 96 of 96 documents with text, 0 empty, and all 3,226 chunks carry one** (was 0 when Ollama was down). Generated by `qwen3.5:4b` at temperature 0 with `think: false` via `python -m chunk.doc_context`, cached document-by-document in `index/doc-context.json`; the run took about 11 minutes (~7 s per document) and is resumable. The 97th file is the 0-page Komori PDF, which has no page to summarise. Sample, unedited: "This document is a doctoral dissertation review report by Professor Yoshino Fumio regarding postharvest vegetable losses…". The chunk export was rebuilt afterwards, so `index/chunks.jsonl` is 3,226 chunks with contexts attached.
- **Chunker cap bug found and fixed by the real corpus (2026-11-14).** OCR pages arrive as one run with no sentence punctuation (the Shrestha scan is a 1,533-character run), so sentence-only splitting could not enforce the 1,500-character cap. `chunk/text.py` now cuts such a run at the last space that fits, and a test proves no word is dropped or split. `chunk/test_text.py` passes **12 of 12**.
- **Chunk suites pass (2026-11-14):** `test_structure.py` 16/16, `test_text.py` 12/12, `test_tables.py` 15/15, `test_meta.py` 11/11, `test_cli.py` 9/9. The table suite now checks the grown corpus: **169 detected tables rendered as 177 chunks**, with the 4 long tables split by row group; the new case proves each part repeats the header row and the row ranges tile the table exactly once. Two stale sample-era assertions (9 tables, one chunk per table) were replaced, not loosened.
- **Retrieval baseline over 97 documents measured (2026-11-15): recall@5 25/26 (96.2%), recall@10 25/26, recall@30 25/26, MRR 0.885**, run by `eval/run_retrieval.py --label 2026-11-14-97docs` over 97 documents / 851 pages / 86 OCR pages; scores in `eval/runs/2026-11-15-2026-11-14-97docs/`. This is the first real retrieval row in `EVAL.md`: every earlier row hands the gold page to the model, so recall@k measured nothing there. Split: dev 17/17 (MRR 0.926), heldout 8/9 (MRR 0.806). **Control run, same method, restricted to the original 5 documents: recall@5 25/26, MRR 0.817** — growing the corpus 20x cost nothing on recall@5 and raised MRR (0.817 → 0.885, because IDF over 851 pages sharpens the ranking). One question is missed entirely at any size: **q015**, "where equations (1) to (4) may be applied", which is a table/equation-reference question, not a wording problem. Citation, numeric and not-found remain unmeasured at this corpus size — that needs the answer run, which waits on the corpus decision, not on the embedder download.

  |---|---|---|---|---|

  | `models/multilingual-e5-small` | `intfloat/multilingual-e5-small` | 471 MB | 493 MB | `1a55775f53449dac10a2bcbc312469fac40b96d53198c407081a831f81c98477` |
  | `models/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` | `sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2` | 91 MB | 92 MB | `53aa51172d142c89d9012cce15ae4d6cc0ca6895895114379cacb4fab128d9db` |
  | `models/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` | `cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2` | 91 MB | 91 MB | `821d1aa69520101d6e0737f78a042ae25b19e5cb9160701909d10434f4aeb0ae` (downloaded 2026-11-22 for D-081; the reranker ships off) |

  **The download was 3.4 GB, not the ~690 MB estimated**, because these repos also ship ONNX and extra weight formats; the folder column is what is on disk after the 2026-11-15 prune, the weights column what inference loads. Benchmark of all 3,226 chunks on CPU with `benchmarks/embeddings/run_embedder_bench.py`, chunk hits mapped back to pages: `bge-small` **23/26 recall@5, 24/26 recall@10, 24/26 recall@30, MRR 0.702, 606 chunks/min**; `e5-small` 23/26, 24/26, 25/26, **0.723**, 585/min; `MiniLM` 18/26, 20/26, 22/26, 0.430, 2,148/min. Full table in `reports/embedder-comparison.md`, per-question rows in `artifacts/step4/embedder-bench/`, three dense-only rows appended to `EVAL.md`. **All three are below the BM25 page baseline (25/26, MRR 0.885)** — partly a granularity effect (BM25 ranks pages, dense ranks chunks, so a gold page's four chunks compete with each other), which is why Step 5 is hybrid.

- **Runtime is Ollama 0.40.16 (2026-11-13), not llama.cpp.** Measured on the same weights through `POST /api/generate`, 3394-token prompt: **499.1 tok/s prompt**, **9.43 tok/s generation** — faster than `llama-server`'s 293.1 and 7.46 on identical input. Projected standard RAG question: 6.8 s prompt + ~13 s for a 120-token answer ≈ **20 s**, so the target is now borderline-feasible rather than missed. `tools/llama.cpp/` (1.9 GB) and the `models/*.gguf` files were deleted; the Ollama blob is untouched.
- Generator measured so far: **`qwen2.5` Q4_K_M, 7.62B, 4.36 GiB** (the owner's existing Ollama model, `ollama 0.40.16`), served by the pinned `llama-server.exe` from a hardlink (`links=2`, no extra disk). The numbers in the three bullets below were taken this way and are kept for comparison; the Ollama figures above supersede them. SHA-256 `2bada8a7450677000f678be90653b85d364de7db25eb5ea54136ada5f3933730`, which is exactly the digest Ollama stores the blob under.
  - `llama-bench` (auto layer split): pp512 **347.8 tok/s**, pp2048 **293.3**, pp4096 **261.0**; tg128 **8.38 tok/s**.
  - 4096 ctx, f16 KV cache: VRAM **3800 MiB of 4096** loaded; 3389-token prompt evaluated in **11.56 s (293 tok/s)**, generation **7.46 tok/s**; answer correct ("540 mm") for a 17-token reply, 13.9 s total.
  - 6144 ctx, `q8_0` KV cache: VRAM **3835 MiB of 4096**; 5397-token prompt in **24.0 s (225 tok/s)**, generation **5.77 tok/s**; answer correct ("about 100 years").
  - Latency projection from those numbers: short follow-up (1k prompt, 100 tokens) ≈ **16 s**, inside the 20 s target; standard RAG (3.4k prompt, 120 tokens) ≈ **28 s**, outside it; deep (5.4k) ≈ **41 s**, inside the 30 s+ target only.
- Parsing profile of the five corpus documents (42 pages): NARAYAN 0 chars / 4 pages / 1 image per page (scan); Ahasan 17 pages, 3 table pages; Das 9 pages, 6 tables; Marahatta 5 pages, 92 figure images; Watanabe 7 pages. `pymupdf4llm` throughput **0.64 s/page (27.07 s / 42 pages)**. `find_tables` finds tables pdfplumber misses (Das p2, p6) but corrupts cells (`69.85` → `6985\n.`); pdfplumber returns clean cells only where it finds a table (Das p9). Unreadable share **9.5% (4/42)**, above the D-020 1-2% threshold.
- A 4226-token prompt was rejected at 4096 context (`exceed_context_size_error`), so the prompt budget must stay under the context window minus the answer; recorded because it is easy to hit with five chunks plus history.
- **Oracle test of `qwen2.5` 7B** (gold page text as context, temperature 0, 30 questions, 3.5-8.8 s per question): **17/30 pass** (dev 12/20, held-out 5/10). recall 26/26 by construction; **citation 14/26 strict**, **19/26** once surrounding quotation marks and the PDF's `East- Rapti` line-break hyphen are ignored — 7 quotes are genuinely paraphrased or truncated (q011, q015, q018, q025, q027, q028, q030); **numeric 22/24** (q016 answered without numbers, q029 said 41 where the gold is 71 growers); not-found (a) 3/3 on dev and (b) 26/26, so refusals and non-refusals are both correct. Raw output and scores in `benchmarks/llm/`.
- **Step 4 table chunker landed, and tables are no longer embedded twice (2026-11-14).** `chunk/tables.py` renders each of the **9 detected tables as one chunk**, with **5 captions recovered** from the text above each bbox, units from the header cells, and footnotes from the blocks below it. Total chunks are now **199 (190 text + 9 table)** rather than 202 text chunks that included table text. `chunk/test_tables.py` passes **14 of 14** cases, and the earlier suites still pass (structure 16/16, text 11/11). Getting the exclusion right took four findings, each now a test: (a) the detected **bbox does not cover the whole table** on Das p2/p9, so a word-overlap rule (≥60% of a block's words are cell words) was added; (b) a **statistics row** — `30.15 / 69.85 / Chi-Square = 5.071, DF = 1, P-Value = 0.024` — is table content but only 7% cell words, so a numeric rule (≥2 of its numbers are cell numbers) was added; (c) that same block turned out to sit **3 pt below table 1's bbox** and is really a footnote, so `chi-square`/`p-value`/`df =` notes are now attached to the table and excluded from prose; (d) the decisive bug — **table blocks were tracked page-locally while the text chunker filters document-locally**, so exclusion silently missed on every page after the first and `69.85` stayed in two text chunks. Blocks now carry `page_block` ids and the translation is explicit. Two of my own test assertions were wrong and were corrected rather than forced: `69.85` legitimately occurs in two different Das tables, and Ahasan's prose quotes its own `36.69` in five paragraphs — so the test checks that each table renders exactly once and that no block feeds both chunkers, which is the property that matters.
- **Step 4 text chunker measured on the real corpus (2026-11-14):** 202 text chunks across 5 documents (Ahasan 85, Das 73, Marahatta 25, Watanabe 13, scan 6), **median body 1,267 characters, mean 1,099, max 1,496, zero over the 1,500 cap**, and `chunk/test_text.py` passes **11 of 11** cases. **103 of 202 chunks (51%) carry a real section path**; the other 99 are the Das, Watanabe and OCR-page chunks, whose documents have no headings, and they say so in the prefix (`(no section headings)`) rather than pretending otherwise. The median is above the 1,000-character target on purpose: pages here are made of ~1,200-1,400 character paragraphs, and splitting a paragraph mid-way costs more in readability than the target buys, so a paragraph is kept whole up to the cap. Overlap is whole sentences only, within a section, and the cap wins over overlap — testing found a 1,469-character body plus a 148-character carry making 1,617, so the carry is now shortened to fit or dropped. **Known and marked:** table cell blocks are still packed as text chunks, so each of the 9 tables is currently embedded twice; `chunk/tables.py` must exclude the bbox blocks, and `TODO(step-04)` sits on it.
- **Step 4 structure module measured on the real corpus (2026-11-14):** `chunk/structure.py` turns the parsed blocks into **42 sections** across 5 documents (Ahasan 16, Das 9, Watanabe 7, Marahatta 6, the NARAYAN scan 4), and `chunk/test_structure.py` passes **16 of 16** cases (exit 0). Headings found: Marahatta 5 (`1. BACKGROUND` … `5. CONCLUSION`), Ahasan 14 (1-5 plus 3.1-3.9). Das, Watanabe and the scan get **one section per page** with `source="none"` rather than an invented path. Three traps are rejected and each has its own case: decimal cells (`21.84`), page-1 affiliation lines (`1 SAARC Meteorological Research Centre (SMRC), Agargaon, Dhaka`), and a list number glued to a sentence (`19. Plants die after flowering …`). The "no chunk crosses a heading" guard is proven both ways: it accepts a single-section chunk and **raises** when two sections are spliced, so the check can actually fail. One gap found and closed while testing: OCR pages have no blocks at all, so the scan produced **0 sections** and would have vanished from chunking — `document_blocks()` now falls back to the page's flattened text, giving the 4 pages one block each.
- **Step 3 now keeps page structure (2026-11-14).** `PageResult.blocks` holds line-preserving text blocks in reading order, next to the flattened `text` that BM25 uses. Measured: **38 of 42 pages carry blocks** (the 4 empty ones are the OCR pages), **median 10 blocks per page**, and the flattened `text` of all 42 pages hashes **identically** before and after (`5b08d662…94fb5`), so nothing that already worked changed. Running headers/footers are removed from `blocks` by line, restricted to each page's first and last block: **137 lines** off Ahasan, **172** off Das, **38** off Marahatta, **50** off Watanabe, **0** off the scan. That restriction matters — matching every line instead deleted **201** lines from Watanabe's 7 figure-heavy pages, because repeated axis labels look like furniture by frequency alone. **5 of 9 table captions are recoverable from these blocks** (`Table-1: List of the El Nino and La Nina years`, `Table 1 Beliefs, Superstitions and Taboos…`); the 4 misses are Das p3 and the three p9 tables. Heading structure is present but irregular, counted exactly: **8 blocks** put a section number and title on one line (5 real Marahatta headings such as `1. BACKGROUND`, 3 affiliation lines on Ahasan page 1 that look identical to a regex), and **34 blocks** put the number alone on the first line with the title beneath — but only **15** of those are headings (Ahasan's 14 plus one glyph-only title), the other 19 being decimal table cells read as numbers (`21.84`/`31.67` on Das p9). **Das and Watanabe have no headings at all**, so Step 4 must chunk them by paragraph with an empty section path, and heading detection must not trust capitalisation (`INtrODUCtION`, `DAtA USED AND MEtHODOLOGY`). Run times varied **48.8-61.3 s** across four identical-code runs (51.6-73.1 pages/min) on this laptop; the variance is machine load, not the change, and the report records whichever run wrote it.
- **Step 3 pipeline, first full run (2026-11-14):** `python -m ingest.cli all` parses 42 pages, 9 tables, 4 OCR pages, 0 unreadable, in **48.8 s = 51.6 pages/min** (extrapolates to **32.2 h per 100k pages**). Table cells checked against each page's own word list: **393/393 numeric tokens identical**, so the `69.85`, `2.51` and `14.07` corruption is gone. Costs: table detection on every page **461 ms/page** (17.5 s of the run), text-only extraction **73.1 pages/min**, OCR **4.4 s/page**. Printed labels were detected for Das (63-71), Marahatta (44, 45, 47) and Watanabe (10, 12, 14, 16); none for Ahasan or the scan. They are recorded only, never used to renumber.
- **Runtime model switched to `qwen3.5:4b`, thinking off (2026-11-14).** `ollama ps` shows it loaded at 3.7 GB, 4096 context, 50%/50% CPU/GPU split. Generated at 13.5-13.7 tok/s with a 1,891-token prompt in the UI (3.3-3.7 s wall). Two behaviours to remember: with thinking left on it returns **empty answers**; even with `think: false` it puts literal `` tags in the content stream, which `ui/server.py`'s `ThinkFilter` now strips. `qwen2.5` 7B stays installed as the fallback (`CHAT_MODEL=qwen2.5`).
- **Step 2 three-model comparison (2026-11-14, owner's instruction):** downloaded `qwen3.5:4b` (3.3 GB) and `phi4-mini:latest` (2.5 GB) from the Ollama registry and measured all three with identical prompts, temp 0, `num_ctx` 4096. **qwen3.5 4B with thinking disabled wins: 23/30** (dev 17/20, held-out 6/10), citation **20/30**, numeric **23/24**, median **4.9 s**, TTFT **3.64 s** at 2,323 prompt tokens, prompt **650.1 tok/s**, generation **10.5 tok/s**, peak VRAM **3,689 MiB**. `phi4-mini` 3.8B: **16/30**, citation 14/30, numeric 23/24, median 2.2 s, TTFT 2.32 s, prompt 976.5 tok/s, generation 22.1 tok/s, VRAM 3,392 MiB. qwen2.5 7B: 18/30, see the bullet below. **Thinking mode returned empty answers on all 30 questions** for qwen3.5 (220-token budget spent on hidden reasoning), so its default row is 4/30 and the usable row uses `think: false` through the native `/api/chat`; this is recorded with every affected artifact.
- **Step 2 finished measurement (2026-11-14):** the oracle was re-run through Ollama on the same 30 questions, temp 0, gold page as context: **18/30** (dev 13/20, held-out 5/10), citation **15/30** (denominator all 30; 4 are not-found), numeric **22/24**, not-found (a) 3/3 and (b) 26/26, median 5.4 s per question (0.9-18.3 s). One question better than the superseded llama.cpp run (17/30). Same run also measured latency and VRAM on the recorded runtime: **TTFT 4.77 s** at 2,345 prompt tokens (0.35 s at 44 tokens), prompt **496.8 tok/s**, generation **8.6 tok/s**, peak **3,505 MiB of 4,096**. A 343-token answer took 44.8 s, so the deep-question case misses 30 s+. Artifacts: `benchmarks/llm/oracle-qwen2.5-7b-ollama.jsonl`, `scores-qwen2.5-7b-ollama/`, `ollama-measure.json`, re-run with `benchmarks/llm/measure-ollama.py`.
- Chosen generator: **`qwen2.5` 7B Q4_K_M**, 4096 ctx, f16 KV cache (owner's choice, 2026-11-13); fallback `qwen2.5-1.5b-instruct` Q4_K_M, which fits entirely on the GPU (1622 MiB, 65.4 tok/s generation in Step 0).
- **OCR in the UI index (2026-11-14):** the 4 NARAYAN pages are OCR'd at index build via `pymupdf4llm.to_markdown(..., use_ocr=True)` (RapidOCR backend), **16-23 s for 4 pages**, and stored in `index/ui-pages.json`. Index rebuild without OCR: ~1 s; with OCR (first time): ~20 s; page count 42, `ocr_pages` 4, `unreadable` now **empty** (was 9.5%). DPI 200 and 300 give the same text, so 200 is kept. **Known OCR damage:** `calcite (5-10 %)` extracts as `(510 %)`, and `qwen2.5` "corrected" it to `5-10%` in one answer — a silent repair the context does not support. Numbers from scanned pages need checking against the PDF.
- Downloaded for Step 2 comparison, from `registry.ollama.ai` (pulled 2026-11-14; Ollama verified each SHA-256 digest on pull, full digests live in Ollama's own manifest store outside this repo): `qwen3.5:4b`, ID `d8b0f5e9760c`, **3.3 GB**, Q4_K_M, 4.2B; `phi4-mini:latest`, ID `78fad5d182a7`, **2.5 GB**, Q4_K_M, 3.8B. Both are text/vision-capable and load in the pinned Ollama 0.40.16; qwen3.5 declares `requires 0.30.0`, satisfied.
- Index cache now stores a corpus signature (name/size/mtime) so replacing a PDF rebuilds instead of searching stale text.
- Parse speed (pages/min): not measured.
- Embedding speed (pages/min): not measured.
